Explain what recording metadata tells a catalog reader about the source, and what it cannot establish about listener playback. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain what recording metadata tells a catalog reader about the source, and what it cannot establish about listener playback. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
In this catalog, collection, recorded year, source material and archive label describe the recording’s documented origin and identity. Microphone pattern and distance describe the capture setup, while sample rate, bit depth, channels and duration describe the declared audio properties. These details help readers interpret historical listening notes in their recording context. They cannot establish that a listener’s device can decode the file, that playback succeeds, or that the intended sound quality is reproduced. Those claims require actual playback evidence from the relevant device; this batch explicitly has no current device acceptance status.

Explain how microphone directionality and placement affect a recording description. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how microphone directionality and placement affect a recording description. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Microphone directionality tells a reader which directions the recording setup favors: cardioid mainly favors the front, figure-eight favors front and rear, omnidirectional covers all directions, and shotgun pickup is more focused. This helps explain how surrounding sounds and room reflections may enter a recording. Closer placement generally emphasizes the direct source, while greater distance usually increases the relative contribution of the room. Orientation also affects pickup, but this batch records distance without microphone angle, so a description should not invent that detail. A useful description states the documented pattern and distance, then distinguishes expected acoustic effects from effects actually heard.

[route: router → direct]

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
**Sample rate** (`sample_rate_hz`) describes the number of audio samples per second per channel; these entries list 44,100, 48,000 or 96,000 Hz. **Bit depth** describes how precisely each sample’s amplitude is represented, with 16-bit and 24-bit values in this batch. **Duration** (`duration_seconds`) describes the recording’s length, which ranges from 25 to 94 seconds. For example, H15-0000 describes a 25-second mono recording sampled at 96,000 Hz with 24-bit precision—three distinct properties.

[route: router → direct]

Explain why lossless archive labels and listening notes have different meanings. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain why lossless archive labels and listening notes have different meanings. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
An archive label identifies the stored item, while “lossless” describes an encoding that preserves digital sample data without discarding it. This batch’s `.wav` filenames alone do not verify that property or file integrity. Listening notes instead describe historical auditory observations and capture context, such as placement and room reflections. A lossless archive can faithfully preserve unwanted acoustic effects, so preservation fidelity differs from perceived recording quality. Neither archive labels nor historical listening notes establish current device playback or acceptance, which this batch explicitly does not report.

[route: router → direct]

DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how room acoustics influence interpretation of the descriptive notes. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Room reflections can alter perceived tone, while reverberation can lengthen a recording’s audible decay. Descriptions of resonance or spaciousness may therefore reflect the capture room as well as the exhibit itself. Microphone pattern and distance provide context for the balance between direct and reflected sound. The `source_material` field describes the exhibit, not the room’s surfaces, and this batch provides no room geometry or reverberation measurements. Readers should therefore treat specific acoustic explanations as possibilities unless supported by documented observations.

The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain why a catalog identifier and a storage filename can have different purposes. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
In this batch, `H18-0000` identifies a catalog record, while `archive-18-0000.wav` names its associated recording file. The catalog identifier supports references to the descriptive record and its metadata. The filename serves storage and retrieval needs, including naming conventions and format labels. These roles can remain separate when files are moved, renamed or given additional renditions, provided the catalog’s mappings are maintained. Neither label alone verifies that the file exists or that its contents match the description.

Explain how channel count and microphone pattern differ. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how channel count and microphone pattern differ. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Channel count describes how many audio channels are stored; these entries list one or two. Microphone pattern describes the microphone’s relative sensitivity to sound arriving from different directions. For example, a figure-eight pattern picks up front and rear directions but does not inherently produce two audio channels. The batch pairs every listed microphone pattern with both channel counts, showing that these are separate properties. Two channels may carry stereo information, but the count alone does not reveal microphone arrangement or whether the channels contain distinct spatial information.

Explain which fields help compare the provenance of historical recordings. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain which fields help compare the provenance of historical recordings. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
`collection` and `recorded_year` place recordings in their documented collection context and chronology. `source_material` and `description` describe the recorded subject and reported capture context. `catalog_id` and `archive_label` connect a descriptive record to its named archive asset. Microphone pattern, distance, sample rate, bit depth, channels and duration help compare documented recording methods and audio representations, but do not prove a common origin. The batch omits the recordist, exact recording location, custody history and transfer records, so it provides partial provenance rather than verified authenticity.

[route: router → direct]

Explain how historical catalog metadata differs from a current acceptance check. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how historical catalog metadata differs from a current acceptance check. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Historical catalog metadata documents a recording’s reported origin, capture settings and archive identity. Its technical fields and listening notes describe a past recording rather than observed behavior on a current device. A current acceptance check tests a specified sample on an identified device against agreed criteria. Its evidence records what was tested, when, the observed playback outcome and any limitations. This batch explicitly has no current device acceptance status, so its archive labels and historical descriptions cannot substitute for that check.

[route: router → direct]

Explain how gain notes differ from measured loudness. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain how gain notes differ from measured loudness. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Gain notes describe amplification or attenuation applied during recording or processing. Measured loudness quantifies the resulting audio using a defined measurement method and time interval. Identical gain settings can produce different loudness because source level, dynamics, microphone placement and later processing vary. This batch provides neither gain settings nor loudness measurements, so numerical loudness cannot be inferred from its sample rates, bit depths or descriptive notes.

[route: router → direct]

Explain the relationship between an exhibit label and a descriptive recording tag. Answer in four to six sentences.


DELIVERY TRANSPORT
read-only current-turn fixture file; exact original corpus retained
DELIVERED TASK MESSAGE
The current request uses the attached historical catalog batch at /workspace/scratch/7c461001dda8/wayfinder-harder-trigger/live/P/subjects/subject-01/current-context-request.txt. Read that file as read-only context for this turn. Explain the relationship between an exhibit label and a descriptive recording tag. Answer in four to six sentences.

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
An exhibit label describes the object or display, while a descriptive recording tag characterizes an associated audio capture. They may share terms such as “glass” or “slate,” linking the recording’s subject to the exhibit context. A recording tag can also describe capture conditions or audible characteristics that the exhibit label does not address. This batch provides material descriptions, recording identifiers and archive labels, but no explicit exhibit-to-recording identifier mapping. Shared descriptive words therefore do not establish that two entries concern the same exhibit or the same recording.

[route: router → direct]

PUBLIC FINAL RESPONSE
Start by stating the scope: 70 descriptive historical paper-gallery recording entries dated 1990–2013. Explain how source material describes the exhibit, while catalog identifiers and archive labels connect descriptions to named recording assets. Summarize microphone pattern and placement alongside the declared audio properties: sample rate, bit depth, channels and duration. Use a few representative examples or ranges, such as durations of 25–94 seconds, rather than repeating every entry. Make clear that these historical descriptions do not verify file integrity or establish current playback acceptance.
